## Evidence Standards Used

- Strongest evidence: fresh path/session-bound MCP runtime attestation, exact function/item boundaries, instruction/decompile behavior, bytes and SHA256, complete low-count xref sets, terminal broad xref counts, stack frames, UDT layouts, constant decoding, and neighboring boundaries.
- Corroboration: current by-* metadata and prose, formal reconstruction blocks, generated CPP/H, matching archived reports, manual coverage, and source-tree structure.
- Negative evidence: zero-function/raw status, zero direct xrefs for retained raw starts, absence of separate vtable/RTTI/import/resource/string/global families, absence of a `RectBoundsLayout` UDT, and exclusion of unrelated neighboring code.
- Confidence is capped below certainty because no original PDB/source tree or compiler-equivalent rebuilt binary is available.

## Positive Evidence Summary

- Exact field accesses prove the four-int `RectBounds` layout and distinguish member functions from pointer-based free helpers.
- Exact contiguous ordering, raw-body bounds, intra-family calls, table refs, and the absence of a competing owner prove the authored family and source order.
- Every accepted body has a human-source formal implementation matching the binary's half-open geometry rules, empty-rectangle conventions, midpoint collapse, signed area, and anchor clipping behavior.
- Dated command `000000025804` independently corroborated the thirty-function family and existing `RectArea` declaration while exposing composition/order and empty-emitter defects; command `000000025640` records the earlier declaration omission. Command `000000025952` verifies those ordinary defects are now closed.
- The strongest inference chain is: exact behavior and calling convention -> member/free/static role -> established UI-core type ownership -> deterministic child nesting -> complete CPP/H output. No alternative explains the same full inventory with fewer artificial files or duplicate declarations.

- Evidence against: none beyond the absence of original source-path symbols.

## IDA Rename / Type / Comment Recommendations

This is a declarative supervisor handoff, not a transaction program. B004 performed no mutation or save. `CANON := E:\NTK\Resources\NexusTK\NexusTK.exe.i64` fixes only the normalized canonical path. Immediately before backup creation or MCP open, the supervisor must obtain a fresh closed-file physical receipt and seal its exact values as `GATE_CANON_PATH`, `GATE_CANON_SIZE`, `GATE_CANON_SHA256`, and `GATE_CANON_LAST_WRITE_UTC`; require `GATE_CANON_PATH == CANON`. That gate-time tuple, not any report-fixed hash, is the one-transaction original baseline dynamically bound through `BACKUP`, pre-save `TX`, promotion expected-current checks, rollback classification, and restoration. Every physical value and every later `BACKUP`/`TX`/saved/verifier/final receipt is established only by separate filesystem receipts; `runtime_attestation` does not return or prove physical IDB size/SHA256. `BACKUP` and `TX` are supervisor-selected absolute paths for one durable backup and one isolated transaction copy. Gate 2B must never open `CANON` for mutation. No evidence-time session, preferred identifier, `idb_list` row, canonical session, attestation-returned alias, prior response, or dated receipt is authority.

### Supported Windows persistence protocol: POWERSHELL_FILE_REPLACE_V1

This is a declarative acceptance contract, not executable report code. The only authorized copy primitive is a direct PowerShell/.NET `System.IO.FileStream` opened with `FileMode.CreateNew`, `FileAccess.Write`, `FileShare.None`, and `FileOptions.WriteThrough`, followed by exact byte-count completion, `Flush(true)`, disposal, and process exit. The only authorized namespace replacement primitive is one direct `[System.IO.File]::Replace(SOURCE,CANON,$null,$true)` call from a fresh PowerShell process. On Windows this is the same-volume `ReplaceFileW` operation exposed by .NET; a null backup argument forbids an implicit side backup, `ignoreMetadataErrors:true` prevents metadata-copy failure from selecting another operation, and a disposable candidate preserves verified `TX` or sealed `BACKUP` as the source receipt. Direct `[System.IO.File]::Delete(PATH)` is authorized only for an exact identity-receipted disposable probe or unconsumed candidate. No copy-overwrite, move, rename, delete-first, second replacement, alternate utility, custom harness, or hash-only fallback is authorized.

Directory-handle `FlushFileBuffers` is neither invoked nor required. Its observed `Access is denied` and the custom harness policy block are historical `DIRECTORY_FLUSH_UNAVAILABLE` evidence, not success and not a reason to attempt either path again. File-byte durability is established only by the direct write-through file handle plus `Flush(true)` and full closure. Namespace-transition acceptance is established only by the one supported `File.Replace` operation plus two consecutive agreeing `CLOSED_NAMESPACE_RECEIPT`s from separate fresh PowerShell processes after every create, cleanup, replacement, and final closure boundary.

A `CLOSED_FILE_RECEIPT` is taken only after all producing database/file handles and processes are closed. Direct approved filesystem queries must record normalized and resolved absolute path, case-folded path, exact parent path and parent file identity, volume identity, regular-file attributes, reparse status, file identity, link count and hardlink names, all streams, byte count, UTC last-write time, and SHA256, followed by a successful read-only `FileStream` open/close with `FileShare.None`. Require one regular non-reparse file, exactly one hardlink at the expected path, only the unnamed data stream, no alias, and agreement with its protected source. A `CLOSED_NAMESPACE_RECEIPT` comes from a fresh independent PowerShell process, re-resolves the sealed parent/volume, performs a case-insensitive exact-name enumeration for `CANON`, `BACKUP`, `TX`, both candidates, every prior candidate/probe name, and operation residue, and includes a fresh `CLOSED_FILE_RECEIPT` for every present role. Two consecutive fresh-process namespace receipts must agree; runtime attestation never substitutes for either physical receipt.

Before `BACKUP`/`TX` creation, MCP open, mutation, or save, run a disposable capability gate in exact sealed `CANON_PARENT`. Choose never-used nonce-bearing `PROBE_SOURCE` and `PROBE_DESTINATION` names absent under two case-insensitive fresh-process enumerations and distinct from all transaction paths. Create both once with distinct nonce payloads through the authorized write-through `CreateNew`/`Flush(true)`/dispose primitive and receipt them. From a new PowerShell process invoke exactly one direct `File.Replace(PROBE_SOURCE,PROBE_DESTINATION,null,true)` call. Two fresh-process receipts must prove source absent, destination present with former-source size/SHA256 and a new regular one-link identity, sealed parent/volume unchanged, and `CANON` unchanged. After exact identity authorization, delete only `PROBE_DESTINATION`; two more fresh-process receipts must prove both probe names absent and `CANON` unchanged. Only then may the fresh `GATE_CANON_*` receipt be sealed and `BACKUP`/`TX` creation begin.

The supported-primitive failure classes are literal. `SUPPORTED_PRIMITIVE_UNAVAILABLE` includes a missing PowerShell/.NET type, method, executable, receipt utility, or fresh-process route. `SUPPORTED_PRIMITIVE_ACCESS_DENIED` includes any authorization or sharing denial from direct `FileStream`, `Flush(true)`, `File.Replace`, `File.Delete`, or receipt access. `SUPPORTED_PRIMITIVE_UNSUPPORTED` includes `PlatformNotSupportedException`, a filesystem/volume that does not support same-volume `File.Replace`, or an unsupported file/stream/identity query. `SUPPORTED_PRIMITIVE_POLICY_BLOCKED` means current policy rejects the direct approved command or fresh process before its primitive is dispatched. `SUPPORTED_PRIMITIVE_AMBIGUOUS` includes timeout, terminated process, missing/partial result, inability to prove whether replacement dispatch began, or disagreement between receipts. During the disposable gate, any such result stops before `BACKUP`, `TX`, MCP open, mutation, or save; retain exact error/receipt evidence, clean only an identity-authorized probe if direct deletion itself remains supported, prove remaining namespace state, and never fall back. A blocked/denied/unsupported probe cleanup leaves the receipt-bound probe in place and stops for supervisor repair with no Gate 2B credit.

After the capability gate, the same classes remain fail-closed. Before replacement dispatch is proven to begin, an unavailable/access-denied/unsupported/policy-blocked direct operation is a pre-consumption failure only when two fresh receipts prove `CANON` still equals its expected-current receipt and the candidate remains unconsumed; delete only that exact candidate if supported, prove no residue, and stop without retry. Once the replacement process/call is dispatched, or dispatch absence cannot be proved, every error, denial, unsupported result, policy termination, timeout, or receipt disagreement is post-attempt ambiguity: spend the sole attempt, perform no cleanup/retry/alternate operation, retain the candidate/namespace evidence as found, retire any canonical route, and use only distinct `DB_ROLLBACK` classification before any BACKUP-only restoration. If direct supported restoration is itself unavailable, denied, unsupported, policy-blocked, or ambiguous, stop with the classified failed `CANON` plus sealed `BACKUP`/`TX` receipts preserved; do not claim restoration, persistence, Gate 2B, checklist, coverage, or lifecycle credit.

### Mandatory isolated startup and public tool surface

1. Pass the complete disposable `POWERSHELL_FILE_REPLACE_V1` capability gate above. Then obtain and seal the fresh separate filesystem `GATE_CANON_*` receipt. Create `BACKUP` and `TX` from exactly those verified bytes through separate authorized write-through `CreateNew`/`Flush(true)`/dispose operations; take two agreeing closed fresh-process receipts after each creation. Require distinct normalized/resolved/case-folded paths and file identities plus exact path/size/SHA equality `BACKUP == GATE_CANON` and `TX == GATE_CANON`, including a recheck that current `CANON` still equals the sealed gate-time tuple. These are filesystem receipts, not runtime-attestation fields. Any copy/collision/flush/closure/receipt failure or supported-primitive failure class hard-stops before MCP open, preflight, or mutation; remove only an exact receipt-bound invalid new role if direct deletion is supported, prove resulting namespace state, and never alter sealed `CANON`/valid `BACKUP`.
2. In the initialized public MCP session, issue a fresh public `tools/list` and require exactly one advertised tool named `runtime_attestation`, zero advertised tools or methods named `runtime/attest`, and exactly one current `idb_save` schema. The accepted `idb_save` input is an object with required string `database`, optional string `path`, and no undeclared fields; its response contract is `{ok:boolean,error?:string,path:string|null}`. Reject any tool-list error, duplicate, alias, partial result, or schema drift. The internal worker RPC `runtime/attest` must never be called, constructed, proxied, or treated as evidence. A tool-list digest, command line, venv path, native-module list, on-disk candidate module, or separately launched interpreter is not loaded-runtime proof.
3. Open only the verified transaction copy with `idb_open({input_path:TX,mode:"force_headless",build_caches:true,init_hexrays:true,run_auto_analysis:false,idle_ttl_sec:3600})`. Require top-level `success:true`, no error-bearing result, a non-null `session`, exact normalized `session.input_path == TX`, and a nonempty returned `session.session_id`; bind `DB := response.session.session_id` without substitution. Every mutation, preflight, refresh, decompile, readback, save, and transaction attestation request uses that exact `DB`, and every transaction-bound path comparison uses exact `TX`.
4. Immediately after open and again immediately before the first preflight or mutation, pair a fresh public `tools/list` result satisfying the exact runtime/save schemas above with public `runtime_attestation({expected_database:DB,expected_canonical_path:TX})`. Attestation does not open, select, rename, or return the authoritative session; it validates only the exact already-returned `DB`/`TX` runtime binding and supplies no physical file receipt.

### Complete mandatory runtime package and separate filesystem receipts

The runtime portion below is indivisible and must be freshly reproduced at every boundary. `ROLE_DB`/`ROLE_PATH` mean the exact current phase pair only: mutation/pre-save `DB`/`TX`, saved-TX verification `DB_VERIFY`/`TX`, normal promoted persistence `DB_FINAL`/`CANON`, failed-state classification `DB_ROLLBACK`/`CANON`, or restored-baseline verification `DB_RESTORE`/`CANON`. Every schema-supplied runtime field must be present, non-null, well-formed, and exactly consistent. Require top-level `schema_version:1`, `ok:true`, and `errors:[]`, plus worker-local `schema_version:1`, `ok:true`, and `errors:[]`. The listener schema has no listener-local `ok` or `errors` fields: no package may require, synthesize, infer, or fail on their absence. Listener closure instead requires its complete actual role, freshness/nonce, endpoint/port ownership, process/parent/generation, source-root, role-manifest, and loaded-module validation below. Require fresh listener and worker `attested_at_utc`/nonce values for the boundary and no missing, stale, ambiguous, duplicate, extra, or unbound active stateful worker/session. Cached or partially repeated output grants no authority. The explicitly labeled filesystem-receipt row is a separate parallel gate and is never attributed to `runtime_attestation`.

After the sole TX save and DB route retirement, exclusively open exact TX read/write with `FileShare.None` and `FileOptions.WriteThrough`, call `Flush(true)` without changing bytes, dispose, and obtain two agreeing fresh-process receipts before binding `TX_SAVED_SHA256`/`TX_SAVED_SIZE`; `CANON` and `BACKUP` must still separately receipt as the dynamic gate-time baseline. Any file-flush or receipt failure prohibits promotion. Runtime attestation proves only that each active role's canonical/input path equals its exact normalized opened path. |

Distinct post-promotion phase roles are mandatory and do not add mutation or protection rows. Every session phase uses a fresh public `tools/list` and the complete indivisible runtime package; for each newly opened role, seal the listener and worker independently against newly accepted baselines for that role and keep those baselines unchanged only within that role. A baseline or attestation from another phase is not authority. The closed-route physical restoration phase has no MCP session and is proved only by route-closure evidence plus separate filesystem receipts.

- `DB_ROLLBACK` is a newly returned, operationally read-only session opened on exact normalized `CANON` only after a post-promotion failure or ambiguity and only after the failed `DB_FINAL`/canonical route is retired. Bind the fresh open's returned ID as `DB_ROLLBACK`; require exact active canonical/input path `CANON`, a distinct `DB_ROLLBACK` listener baseline and worker baseline, and three separate fresh packages immediately post-open, immediately pre-read, and immediately post-read. Before open and after the post-read package, obtain separate physical canonical path/size/SHA256 receipts. Between the pre-read and post-read packages, reread every entity addressed by the 78 action rows and all 52 protections, classify each as verified saved-TX state, original baseline, or failed/ambiguous other state, and retain exact semantic evidence. `DB_ROLLBACK` may classify only; it has no mutation, save, physical replacement, promotion, lifecycle, or success-credit authority.
- Physical restoration is a separate supervisor-owned closed-route operation, never an action by `DB_ROLLBACK`. Before touching `CANON`, retire `DB_ROLLBACK` and every other canonical worker/redirector/session, independently prove no active route owns `CANON`, retain the failed canonical receipt, and freshly verify the separately sealed `BACKUP` path/size/SHA256 still equals the dynamically sealed `GATE_CANON_*` original baseline. Execute only the complete sealed exact-parent `RESTORE_CANDIDATE` contract below, sourced solely from those freshly reverified `BACKUP` bytes. Neither `TX` nor promoted/saved-TX bytes may substitute as the restoration source, and no direct copy-to-`CANON` or fallback replacement is authorized.
- `DB_RESTORE` is a second newly returned, operationally read-only session opened on restored exact normalized `CANON` only after the closed-route replacement and restored physical receipt pass. Bind only that fresh open's returned ID as `DB_RESTORE`; require exact active canonical/input path `CANON`, a distinct `DB_RESTORE` listener baseline and worker baseline, and three separate fresh packages immediately post-open, immediately pre-read, and immediately post-read. Before open and after the post-read package, obtain separate restored canonical path/size/SHA256 receipts equal to the sealed `BACKUP` baseline. Between pre-read and post-read, reread the complete original pre-transaction baseline for every entity addressed by all 78 action rows and all 52 protections; any saved-action residue, semantic drift, physical mismatch, or package failure leaves restoration unverified and grants no persistence/lifecycle credit. `DB_RESTORE` has no mutation, save, promotion, or lifecycle authority.

Session separation is absolute whenever a phase occurs: `DB`, `DB_VERIFY`, `DB_FINAL`, `DB_ROLLBACK`, and `DB_RESTORE` each require their own newly returned session ID from that phase's applicable open, and no two roles may share an ID. None may be retained, rebound, aliased, substituted, or reused for another phase, and no role-specific module baseline may be borrowed across those phases.

### Sealed exact-parent promotion and restoration candidates

`CANON_PARENT := E:\NTK\Resources\NexusTK` is the normalized absolute exact parent of `CANON`. Before either replacement path, obtain and seal a separate read-only filesystem identity for that exact directory, including normalized/resolved/case-folded path, volume identity, directory file identity, and reparse status; require every path component from the volume root through `CANON_PARENT` to be the expected real directory and not a reparse point, junction, symlink, mount, alias, or alternate path. Recheck the same sealed parent identity in two fresh-process namespace receipts immediately before candidate creation, immediately before `File.Replace`, and after replacement or cleanup. No directory handle is opened or flushed. Parent drift or inability to obtain two agreeing receipts hard-stops with no replacement authority.

`PROMOTION_CANDIDATE` and `RESTORE_CANDIDATE` are physical-file labels only and do not add database roles: the role set remains exactly `DB`, `DB_VERIFY`, `DB_FINAL`, `DB_ROLLBACK`, and `DB_RESTORE`. For each applicable operation, select a never-before-used unique filename directly inside sealed `CANON_PARENT` and create it with collision-failing create-new/no-overwrite semantics. Maintain a transaction-local ledger of every candidate name/path attempted and require the new normalized path to be distinct from `CANON`, `BACKUP`, `TX`, the other named candidate, and every prior candidate. Require candidate and `CANON` to have the same volume identity; reject any cross-volume route, path alias, reparse point, symlink, junction, hardlink, alternate data stream, or filename containing an ADS suffix. The created candidate must be a new regular file with a file identity distinct from `CANON`, `BACKUP`, `TX`, and all prior candidates, link count exactly one, and exactly the unnamed default data stream. Collision, overwrite, delete-first, path reuse, alias resolution, extra stream, extra link, or fallback naming hard-stops.

- Promotion source and route gate: only the separately receipted saved `TX` whose normalized path/size/SHA256 equals `TX`/`TX_SAVED_SIZE`/`TX_SAVED_SHA256` may supply `PROMOTION_CANDIDATE`. After the complete `DB_VERIFY` readback and final pre-promotion package, retire `DB_VERIFY` and every route/session/worker/redirector that can own `TX`, `CANON`, or the candidate path; prove route retirement independently, then reprove saved `TX`, sealed `BACKUP` equal to `GATE_CANON_*`, unchanged gate-time-baseline `CANON`, and sealed `CANON_PARENT` from fresh filesystem receipts before reading source bytes. The expected-current receipt for guarded promotion is that freshly reverified dynamic gate-time `CANON` receipt.
- Restoration source and route gate: only freshly reverified sealed `BACKUP` bytes at its exact normalized path and dynamic `GATE_CANON_SIZE`/`GATE_CANON_SHA256` may supply `RESTORE_CANDIDATE`. Retire `DB_ROLLBACK` and every route/session/worker/redirector that can own `CANON`, `BACKUP`, or the candidate path; independently prove route retirement, retain and freshly reprove the failed/ambiguous canonical receipt classified by `DB_ROLLBACK`, and reprove sealed `BACKUP` plus sealed `CANON_PARENT` immediately before reading source bytes. The expected-current receipt for guarded restoration is that exact freshly reverified failed/ambiguous `CANON` receipt. `TX`, saved-TX bytes, promoted bytes, and any other copy are forbidden restoration sources.
- Candidate construction and independent receipt: copy only the permitted protected source into the collision-failing create-new candidate through the authorized direct write-through `FileStream`; require exact source byte count, call `Flush(true)`, dispose every source/candidate handle, and exit the producing process. Two consecutive fresh-process receipts must independently re-resolve and reopen the candidate, proving exact parent/volume/file identity, regular non-reparse one-link unnamed-stream state, exact expected size/SHA256 (`TX_SAVED_SIZE`/`TX_SAVED_SHA256` for promotion or sealed `BACKUP` size/SHA256 for restoration), source unchanged, and no unrelated role/residue drift. Any short write, flush/closure failure, supported-primitive failure, or receipt mismatch is pre-consumption: delete only the exact receipt-bound candidate if supported, require two receipts proving its absence and unchanged `CANON`/`BACKUP`, and stop. Cleanup unavailable/access-denied/unsupported/policy-blocked leaves the candidate receipted in place and stops with no retry or credit.
- Expected-current-bound atomic consumption and final receipt: immediately before dispatch, two fresh-process receipts must reprove route retirement, sealed parent/volume identity, the protected source, the closed candidate, and exact current `CANON` equality to the operation's expected-current normalized/resolved path, volume/file identity, size, SHA256, streams, links, attributes, and last-write receipt; a successful exclusive `FileShare.None` open/close must prove no remaining holder. From one new PowerShell process invoke exactly one direct same-volume `[System.IO.File]::Replace(CANDIDATE,CANON,$null,$true)` call. The candidate is the only replacement source; verified TX/BACKUP are not consumed. No copy/delete, delete/rename, move, unguarded overwrite, cross-volume path, direct protected-source replacement, retry, alternate primitive, candidate recreation, or non-atomic fallback exists. After dispatch, two new-process namespace receipts must prove candidate absent, exact `CANON` equality to the candidate/protected-source size/SHA256 with a regular non-reparse one-link unnamed-stream identity, sealed parent/volume unchanged, protected TX/BACKUP unchanged, the other candidate absent, and no case-folded or operation residue. Exclusively reopen exact `CANON` read/write with `FileShare.None` and `FileOptions.WriteThrough`, call `Flush(true)` without byte changes, dispose, then take two final agreeing receipts. Promotion may proceed to new read-only `DB_FINAL` only after this closure; restoration may proceed to new read-only `DB_RESTORE` only after its closure. Any exception or supported-primitive failure after dispatch, any inability to prove dispatch absence, or any post-call/flush/receipt disagreement is post-attempt ambiguity and invokes `DB_ROLLBACK`/BACKUP-only recovery without retry or cleanup.

### Repeated boundary schedule and failure disposition

A bounded stage means one of the 78 mutation rows together with its required refresh/decompile/complete readback tail, or one of the 52 independent no-action protection-row rereads. The fresh public `tools/list` plus complete attestation package is an additional protection gate, not a mutation row or no-action protection row, and does not change the 78/52 topology.

| Boundary | Mandatory fresh package and permitted next step |
| --- | --- |
| Isolated open/startup | Pass immediately after `idb_open(TX)` and again immediately before any preflight or mutation, using returned `DB` and exact `TX`. Only then may bounded preflight begin. |
| Every bounded protection/mutation stage | Pass immediately before the row's first read/prestate or stateful mutation and again after its complete protected readback, before the next row. This independently brackets I67 and I68 and also requires a fresh post-stage package after each define, rename, type, stack, comment, and protection stage. |
| Sole save | After all 78 mutation rows and all 52 protections pass, run a fresh runtime package immediately before the sole exact `idb_save({database:DB,path:TX}) -> {ok,error?,path}`. Require `ok:true`, absent or empty `error`, and returned normalized `path == TX`; any other response is save failure. Retire DB's route and every TX handle/process, exclusively open exact TX read/write with `FileShare.None`/`FileOptions.WriteThrough`, call `Flush(true)` without changing bytes, dispose, and obtain two agreeing fresh-process physical TX receipts. Bind only those receipts' hash/size as `TX_SAVED_SHA256`/`TX_SAVED_SIZE`. Any unavailable/access-denied/unsupported/policy-blocked/ambiguous direct primitive after save prohibits verifier credit and promotion while leaving `CANON`/`BACKUP` unchanged. |
| Verifier/reopen | Freshly reopen the exact separately receipted saved `TX` for readback, bind only that open's returned session as `DB_VERIFY`, and pass the runtime package with `expected_database:DB_VERIFY`/`expected_canonical_path:TX` immediately after open, before persistence readback, and again after the complete 78-action/52-protection persistence readback. A separate verifier filesystem receipt must equal `TX_SAVED_SHA256`/`TX_SAVED_SIZE`. `DB_VERIFY` is readback-only and grants no mutation or save. |
| Pre-promotion and exact-parent candidate | Immediately before any supervisor-authorized promotion, pass a fresh `DB_VERIFY`/`TX` runtime package, require separate `TX` receipt equality to `TX_SAVED_SHA256`/`TX_SAVED_SIZE`, separate `CANON`/`BACKUP` baseline receipts, and complete persisted state. Then retire the verifier and all owning routes, reprove protected source/parent/current-canonical receipts, and execute `POWERSHELL_FILE_REPLACE_V1`: collision-failing write-through `CreateNew`/`Flush(true)` candidate, closure, two agreeing receipts, one direct expected-current-bound `File.Replace` dispatch, post-call canonical `Flush(true)`/closure, two agreeing canonical/namespace receipts, and exact-parent no-residue proof. The report itself grants no promotion authority. |
| Post-promotion failed/ambiguous classification | Retire the failed `DB_FINAL`/canonical route, freshly open exact `CANON` read-only, bind only the newly returned session as `DB_ROLLBACK`, establish its own listener and worker baselines, and pass separate fresh post-open, pre-read, and post-read packages. Complete the failed-state 78-action/52-protection semantic classification between the read packages and take separate pre-open/post-read physical canonical receipts. `DB_ROLLBACK` has classification authority only. |
| Closed-route exact-parent physical restore from sealed BACKUP | Retire `DB_ROLLBACK` and every owning route, prove no active session/worker owns `CANON`, `BACKUP`, or the candidate path, freshly receipt sealed `BACKUP` at the dynamic `GATE_CANON_*` original baseline, and retain/reprove the failed canonical expected-current receipt. Execute the same independent `POWERSHELL_FILE_REPLACE_V1` create-new/write-through/`Flush(true)`/close/two-receipt candidate contract and one direct expected-current-bound `File.Replace` dispatch, sourced only from `BACKUP`, followed by canonical file flush/closure, two agreeing restored-canonical/namespace receipts, and exact-parent no-residue proof. No MCP session performs or proves this disk operation. |
| Post-restore original-baseline verification | Only after physical restoration receipts pass, freshly open restored exact `CANON` read-only, bind only the newly returned session as `DB_RESTORE`, establish its own listener and worker baselines, and pass separate fresh post-open, pre-read, and post-read packages. Between read packages require the complete dynamically sealed gate-time pre-transaction baseline across all 78 action-row entities and 52 protections, with separate pre-open/post-read restored canonical receipts equal to sealed `BACKUP` and `GATE_CANON_*`. `DB_RESTORE` has verification authority only. |
| Final persistence | After any separately authorized promotion, freshly reopen `CANON` read-only, bind only its newly returned session as `DB_FINAL`, establish that role's own listener and worker baselines, and pass the runtime package with exact `DB_FINAL`/`CANON` immediately after open, before final persisted-state readback, and again after it. A separate final canonical filesystem receipt must equal the verified saved TX identity; a separate `BACKUP` receipt must still equal the dynamic `GATE_CANON_*` original baseline. Require all 78 action results and 52 protections exact and no active mutation route. `DB_FINAL` cannot be reused as `DB_ROLLBACK` or `DB_RESTORE`. |

Any package or supported-primitive failure before save means save nothing, retire only the isolated redirector/worker, dispose only receipt-authorized TX artifacts, and leave `CANON`/sealed `BACKUP` byte-identical. A failure after TX save but before replacement dispatch means promote nothing and keep canonical/backup untouched; only new read-only `DB_VERIFY` may inspect a valid receipted saved TX. Any pre-dispatch candidate failure requires identity-authorized direct deletion when supported and two residue-free receipts with `CANON` still equal its expected-current receipt; cleanup blocked/denied/unsupported leaves the candidate in place and stops with no retry or credit. Any error once `File.Replace` dispatch begins or cannot be disproved is post-attempt ambiguity: do not delete/retry/recreate, retire `DB_FINAL` if present, and use only new read-only `DB_ROLLBACK` for exact failed-canonical classification. When classification requires restoration, close every canonical route and execute only one independent BACKUP-sourced `RESTORE_CANDIDATE` `File.Replace` attempt; then use only distinct new read-only `DB_RESTORE` for complete original 78-action/52-protection and physical receipt verification. If restoration or its direct primitives are unavailable, access-denied, unsupported, policy-blocked, timed out, or ambiguous, preserve classified `CANON` and sealed `BACKUP`/`TX` receipts and stop. No failure branch grants persistence, Gate 2B, checklist, coverage, or lifecycle credit.

After that isolated-copy binding, `AR`, `AP`, `FR`, and `FP` mean address regular, address repeatable, function regular, and function repeatable comments. Every channel is independent. Every action requires a fresh collision/prestate reread, the exact stage-local post-action readback specified below, and the complete before/after boundary package; only the supervisor may save the returned transaction-copy `DB`, never a canonical session.

Every pure function rename below first requires the destination-collision query, then the rename action, and then the immediate exact-address `inspect_items` persisted-name proof before any cache refresh, decompile, type, comment, or other mutation. Lookup and decompiler caches are allowed to remain stale only during that bounded immediate proof. Every rename or type action then has the mandatory exact-address cache-refresh tail below: call `force_recompile({database:DB,items:{addr:ADDR}})` and require one non-error invalidation result for that exact function entry, then call `decompile({database:DB,addr:ADDR,include_addresses:false})` and require non-error regenerated pseudocode for that same function. Only after those two calls may the supervisor perform the action row's complete literal persisted-IDB/public readback: exact range and name, persisted function or stack type, public `inspect_items` presentation, complete physical frame, independent AR/AP/FR/FP channels, exact body bytes/hash, and exact xref count/set/digest. A comment action follows the successful post-type refresh/readback and is verified independently by `get_comments` plus the complete protected-state readback. `force_recompile` invalidates only the Hex-Rays cache; it is nonpersistent, is not an IDB mutation receipt, and never substitutes for persisted-IDB verification or final post-save reread. No unlisted mutation may occur between an action and its prescribed verification. Any stale presentation after the required refresh or any other mismatch fails closed before the next action and before save.

### Literal raw-function define stages

The 2026-08-18 bounded read-only repair pass reconfirmed that every start is a one-byte unnamed code item with empty public type, no function, AR/AP absent, FR/FP not applicable, `stack_frame` returning `No function found`, and zero inbound xrefs. Exact prologues, stack allocation, returns, body hashes, and neighboring table/alignment boundaries now support deterministic fail-closed `define_func` contracts. The immediate define frames below intentionally contain only IDA's represented saved-register/return rows; source arguments are added only by the independent type stage. No decompile or cache action occurs during the immediate define readback, so persisted type absence and the public empty type are established before any decompiler-derived presentation exists.

### Raw-function pure rename stages

Each rename is independently collision-gated and runs only after its exact I01-I10 immediate define/type-absence readback. Immediately after the pure rename and before any cache refresh, decompile, type, comment, or other mutation, `inspect_items({database:DB,addrs:[ADDR]})` must return exactly one item at the literal row address/range with the proposed name and an empty persisted type; that exact-address result is the required persisted-name proof. Lookup and decompiler presentation may remain stale only until the mandatory rename cache-refresh tail completes. The complete row readback then requires the refreshed unique lookup and non-error Hex-Rays body under the new name, while every field except the function name remains exact.

### Protected no-change entities

These are independent no-action protections, not grouped mutation rows. Each row serializes its own dated physical prestate and exact unchanged Gate 2B readback. Data/alignment rows have no function object, so FR/FP are not applicable; AR/AP are stated independently. The supervisor must reread every row in its fresh attested session and fail closed on any mismatch.

```cpp
#include "RectBounds.h"

bool RectBounds::IsEmptyOrInvalid() const
{
    return bottom <= top || right <= left;
}

bool RectBounds::HasSameSizeAs(const RectBounds *bounds) const
{
    return right - left == bounds->right - bounds->left &&
           bottom - top == bounds->bottom - bounds->top;
}

bool RectBounds::ContainsPoint(int y, int x) const
{
    return left <= x && x < right &&
           top <= y && y < bottom;
}

bool RectBounds::ContainsRect(const RectBounds *bounds) const
{
    if (bounds->IsEmptyOrInvalid()) {
        return true;
    }

    if (IsEmptyOrInvalid()) {
        return false;
    }

    return left <= bounds->left &&
           bounds->right <= right &&
           top <= bounds->top &&
           bounds->bottom <= bottom;
}

bool RectBounds::IntersectInto(const RectBounds *bounds, RectBounds *outBounds) const
{
    if (IsEmptyOrInvalid() || bounds->IsEmptyOrInvalid()) {
        if (outBounds != 0) {
            outBounds->left = 0;
            outBounds->top = 0;
            outBounds->right = 0;
            outBounds->bottom = 0;
        }
        return false;
    }

    RectBounds result;
    result.left = (left <= bounds->left) ? bounds->left : left;
    result.top = (top <= bounds->top) ? bounds->top : top;
    result.right = (right <= bounds->right) ? right : bounds->right;
    result.bottom = (bottom <= bounds->bottom) ? bottom : bounds->bottom;

    if (result.IsEmptyOrInvalid()) {
        if (outBounds != 0) {
            outBounds->left = 0;
            outBounds->top = 0;
            outBounds->right = 0;
            outBounds->bottom = 0;
        }
        return false;
    }

    if (outBounds != 0) {
        *outBounds = result;
    }
    return true;
}

void RectBounds::UnionInto(const RectBounds *bounds, RectBounds *outBounds) const
{
    if (IsEmptyOrInvalid()) {
        *outBounds = *bounds;
        return;
    }

    if (bounds->IsEmptyOrInvalid()) {
        *outBounds = *this;
        return;
    }

    outBounds->left = (left <= bounds->left) ? left : bounds->left;
    outBounds->top = (top <= bounds->top) ? top : bounds->top;
    outBounds->right = (right <= bounds->right) ? bounds->right : right;
    outBounds->bottom = (bottom <= bounds->bottom) ? bounds->bottom : bottom;
}

void RectBounds::SetLTRB(int leftValue, int topValue, int rightValue, int bottomValue)
{
    left = leftValue;
    top = topValue;
    right = rightValue;
    bottom = bottomValue;
}

void RectBounds::Offset(int dx, int dy)
{
    left += dx;
    top += dy;
    right += dx;
    bottom += dy;
}

void RectBounds::Inset(int dx, int dy)
{
    left += dx;
    right -= dx;
    top += dy;
    bottom -= dy;

    if (left > right) {
        left = right = (left + right) / 2;
    }

    if (top > bottom) {
        top = bottom = (top + bottom) / 2;
    }
}

bool RectBounds::IntersectWith(const RectBounds *bounds)
{
    if (bottom <= top || right <= left ||
        bounds->bottom <= bounds->top || bounds->right <= bounds->left) {
        left = 0;
        top = 0;
        right = 0;
        bottom = 0;
        return false;
    }

    const int newLeft = (left <= bounds->left) ? bounds->left : left;
    const int newTop = (top <= bounds->top) ? bounds->top : top;
    const int newRight = (right <= bounds->right) ? right : bounds->right;
    const int newBottom = (bottom <= bounds->bottom) ? bottom : bounds->bottom;

    if (newBottom <= newTop || newRight <= newLeft) {
        left = 0;
        top = 0;
        right = 0;
        bottom = 0;
        return false;
    }

    left = newLeft;
    top = newTop;
    right = newRight;
    bottom = newBottom;
    return true;
}

RectBounds *RectBounds::UnionWith(const RectBounds *bounds)
{
    if (IsEmptyOrInvalid()) {
        *this = *bounds;
        return this;
    }

    if (!bounds->IsEmptyOrInvalid()) {
        if (bounds->top < top) {
            top = bounds->top;
        }
        if (bounds->left < left) {
            left = bounds->left;
        }
        if (bottom < bounds->bottom) {
            bottom = bounds->bottom;
        }
        if (right < bounds->right) {
            right = bounds->right;
        }
    }

    return this;
}

void RectBounds::ClampWithin(int leftLimit, int topLimit, int rightLimit, int bottomLimit)
{
    if (left < leftLimit) {
        right += leftLimit - left;
        left = leftLimit;
    } else if (rightLimit < right) {
        left += rightLimit - right;
        right = rightLimit;
    }

    if (top < topLimit) {
        bottom += topLimit - top;
        top = topLimit;
    } else if (bottomLimit < bottom) {
        top += bottomLimit - bottom;
        bottom = bottomLimit;
    }
}

RectBounds *RectBounds::InsetByQuarter(RectBounds *outBounds) const
{
    const float quarterWidth = static_cast<float>(this->right - this->left) * 0.25f;
    const float quarterHeight = static_cast<float>(this->bottom - this->top) * 0.25f;

    outBounds->left = static_cast<int>(static_cast<float>(this->left) + quarterWidth + 0.5f);
    outBounds->top = static_cast<int>(static_cast<float>(this->top) + quarterHeight + 0.5f);
    outBounds->right = static_cast<int>(static_cast<float>(this->right) - quarterWidth);
    outBounds->bottom = static_cast<int>(static_cast<float>(this->bottom) - quarterHeight);
    return outBounds;
}

RectBounds *RectBounds::OutsetByHalf(RectBounds *outBounds) const
{
    const float halfWidth = static_cast<float>(this->right - this->left) * 0.5f;
    const float halfHeight = static_cast<float>(this->bottom - this->top) * 0.5f;

    outBounds->left = static_cast<int>(static_cast<float>(this->left) - halfWidth - 0.5f);
    outBounds->top = static_cast<int>(static_cast<float>(this->top) - halfHeight - 0.5f);
    outBounds->right = static_cast<int>(static_cast<float>(this->right) + halfWidth);
    outBounds->bottom = static_cast<int>(static_cast<float>(this->bottom) + halfHeight);
    return outBounds;
}

void InitPointPair(Point *point, int y, int x)
{
    point->y = y;
    point->x = x;
}

void InitRectBounds(RectBounds *bounds, int left, int top, int right, int bottom)
{
    bounds->left = left;
    bounds->top = top;
    bounds->right = right;
    bounds->bottom = bottom;
}

void InitRectBoundsFromSize(RectBounds *bounds, int left, int top, int width, int height)
{
    bounds->left = left;
    bounds->top = top;
    bounds->right = left + width;
    bounds->bottom = top + height;
}

int RectArea(const RectBounds *bounds)
{
    return (bounds->right - bounds->left) * (bounds->bottom - bounds->top);
}

bool IntersectRects(const RectBounds *firstBounds, const RectBounds *secondBounds, RectBounds *outBounds)
{
    if (firstBounds->bottom <= firstBounds->top ||
        firstBounds->right <= firstBounds->left ||
        secondBounds->bottom <= secondBounds->top ||
        secondBounds->right <= secondBounds->left) {
        if (outBounds != 0) {
            InitRectBounds(outBounds, 0, 0, 0, 0);
        }
        return false;
    }

    RectBounds result;
    result.left = (firstBounds->left <= secondBounds->left) ? secondBounds->left : firstBounds->left;
    result.top = (firstBounds->top <= secondBounds->top) ? secondBounds->top : firstBounds->top;
    result.right = (firstBounds->right <= secondBounds->right) ? firstBounds->right : secondBounds->right;
    result.bottom = (firstBounds->bottom <= secondBounds->bottom) ? firstBounds->bottom : secondBounds->bottom;

    if (result.bottom <= result.top || result.right <= result.left) {
        if (outBounds != 0) {
            InitRectBounds(outBounds, 0, 0, 0, 0);
        }
        return false;
    }

    if (outBounds != 0) {
        *outBounds = result;
    }
    return true;
}

void UnionRects(const RectBounds *firstBounds, const RectBounds *secondBounds, RectBounds *outBounds)
{
    if (firstBounds->bottom <= firstBounds->top ||
        firstBounds->right <= firstBounds->left) {
        *outBounds = *secondBounds;
        return;
    }

    if (secondBounds->bottom <= secondBounds->top ||
        secondBounds->right <= secondBounds->left) {
        *outBounds = *firstBounds;
        return;
    }

    outBounds->left = (firstBounds->left <= secondBounds->left) ? firstBounds->left : secondBounds->left;
    outBounds->top = (firstBounds->top <= secondBounds->top) ? firstBounds->top : secondBounds->top;
    outBounds->right = (firstBounds->right <= secondBounds->right) ? secondBounds->right : firstBounds->right;
    outBounds->bottom = (firstBounds->bottom <= secondBounds->bottom) ? secondBounds->bottom : firstBounds->bottom;
}

void OffsetRect(RectBounds *bounds, int dx, int dy)
{
    bounds->left += dx;
    bounds->top += dy;
    bounds->right += dx;
    bounds->bottom += dy;
}

void InsetRect(RectBounds *bounds, int dx, int dy)
{
    bounds->left += dx;
    bounds->right -= dx;
    bounds->top += dy;
    bounds->bottom -= dy;

    if (bounds->left > bounds->right) {
        const int midpoint = (bounds->left + bounds->right) / 2;
        bounds->left = midpoint;
        bounds->right = midpoint;
    }

    if (bounds->top > bounds->bottom) {
        const int midpoint = (bounds->top + bounds->bottom) / 2;
        bounds->top = midpoint;
        bounds->bottom = midpoint;
    }
}

bool PointInRect(int y, int x, const RectBounds *bounds)
{
    return bounds->left <= x && x < bounds->right &&
           bounds->top <= y && y < bounds->bottom;
}

bool IsRectEmptyOrInvalid(const RectBounds *bounds)
{
    return bounds->bottom <= bounds->top || bounds->right <= bounds->left;
}

bool RectsEquivalent(const RectBounds *firstBounds, const RectBounds *secondBounds)
{
    if (IsRectEmptyOrInvalid(firstBounds) && IsRectEmptyOrInvalid(secondBounds)) {
        return true;
    }

    return firstBounds->left == secondBounds->left &&
           firstBounds->top == secondBounds->top &&
           firstBounds->right == secondBounds->right &&
           firstBounds->bottom == secondBounds->bottom;
}

bool RectContainsRect(const RectBounds *innerBounds, const RectBounds *outerBounds)
{
    if (IsRectEmptyOrInvalid(innerBounds)) {
        return true;
    }

    if (IsRectEmptyOrInvalid(outerBounds)) {
        return false;
    }

    return outerBounds->left <= innerBounds->left &&
           innerBounds->right <= outerBounds->right &&
           outerBounds->top <= innerBounds->top &&
           innerBounds->bottom <= outerBounds->bottom;
}

static void AdjustRectAnchorAxis(int anchor, int offset, int *firstLow, int *firstHigh, int *secondLow, int *secondHigh);

static int Transpose3x3AnchorIndex(int anchor)
{
    switch (anchor) {
    case 0:
        return 0;
    case 1:
        return 3;
    case 2:
        return 6;
    case 3:
        return 1;
    case 4:
        return 4;
    case 5:
        return 7;
    case 6:
        return 2;
    case 7:
        return 5;
    case 8:
        return 8;
    default:
        return anchor;
    }
}

static void AdjustRectPairForAnchor(const RectBounds *firstBounds, const RectBounds *secondBounds, int anchor, RectBounds *outFirstBounds, RectBounds *outSecondBounds)
{
    *outFirstBounds = *firstBounds;
    *outSecondBounds = *secondBounds;

    AdjustRectAnchorAxis(anchor, 0, &outFirstBounds->left, &outFirstBounds->right, &outSecondBounds->left, &outSecondBounds->right);

    const int transposedAnchor = Transpose3x3AnchorIndex(anchor);
    AdjustRectAnchorAxis(transposedAnchor, 0, &outFirstBounds->top, &outFirstBounds->bottom, &outSecondBounds->top, &outSecondBounds->bottom);
}

static void AdjustRectPairForAnchorWithOffsets(const RectBounds *firstBounds, const RectBounds *secondBounds, int anchor, int horizontalOffset, int verticalOffset, RectBounds *outFirstBounds, RectBounds *outSecondBounds)
{
    *outFirstBounds = *firstBounds;
    *outSecondBounds = *secondBounds;

    AdjustRectAnchorAxis(anchor, horizontalOffset, &outFirstBounds->left, &outFirstBounds->right, &outSecondBounds->left, &outSecondBounds->right);

    const int transposedAnchor = Transpose3x3AnchorIndex(anchor);
    AdjustRectAnchorAxis(transposedAnchor, verticalOffset, &outFirstBounds->top, &outFirstBounds->bottom, &outSecondBounds->top, &outSecondBounds->bottom);
}

static void AdjustRectAnchorAxis(int anchor, int offset, int *firstLow, int *firstHigh, int *secondLow, int *secondHigh)
{
    const int originalSecondLow = *secondLow;
    const int originalSecondHigh = *secondHigh;
    const int secondSpan = originalSecondHigh - originalSecondLow;
    const int originalFirstLow = *firstLow;
    const int firstSpan = *firstHigh - originalFirstLow;
    const int spanDelta = firstSpan - secondSpan;

    int anchorOffset;
    switch (anchor) {
    case 0:
    case 3:
    case 6:
        anchorOffset = 0;
        break;
    case 1:
    case 4:
    case 7:
        anchorOffset = spanDelta / 2;
        break;
    case 2:
    case 5:
    case 8:
        anchorOffset = spanDelta;
        break;
    default:
        return;
    }

    if (offset < anchorOffset - firstSpan) {
        *firstLow = *firstHigh;
        *secondHigh -= secondSpan;
        return;
    }

    if (spanDelta <= 0) {
        if (offset < anchorOffset) {
            *firstLow = anchorOffset + originalFirstLow - offset;
            *secondHigh += offset + spanDelta - anchorOffset;
            return;
        }

        if (offset < anchorOffset - spanDelta) {
            *secondLow = offset + originalSecondLow - anchorOffset;
            *secondHigh += offset + spanDelta - anchorOffset;
            return;
        }
    } else {
        if (offset < anchorOffset - spanDelta) {
            *firstLow = anchorOffset + originalFirstLow - offset;
            *secondHigh += offset + spanDelta - anchorOffset;
            return;
        }

        if (offset < anchorOffset) {
            *firstLow = anchorOffset + originalFirstLow - offset;
            *firstHigh += anchorOffset - spanDelta - offset;
            return;
        }
    }

    if (offset >= anchorOffset + secondSpan) {
        *secondLow = originalSecondHigh;
        *firstHigh -= firstSpan;
    } else {
        *secondLow = offset + originalSecondLow - anchorOffset;
        *firstHigh += anchorOffset - spanDelta - offset;
    }
}
```

#ifndef NEXUSTK_UI_CORE_RECTBOUNDS_H
#define NEXUSTK_UI_CORE_RECTBOUNDS_H

struct Point
{
    int y;
    int x;
};

struct RectBounds
{
    int left;
    int top;
    int right;
    int bottom;

    bool IsEmptyOrInvalid() const;
    bool HasSameSizeAs(const RectBounds *bounds) const;
    bool ContainsPoint(int y, int x) const;
    bool ContainsRect(const RectBounds *bounds) const;
    bool IntersectInto(const RectBounds *bounds, RectBounds *outBounds) const;
    void UnionInto(const RectBounds *bounds, RectBounds *outBounds) const;
    void SetLTRB(int leftValue, int topValue, int rightValue, int bottomValue);
    void Offset(int dx, int dy);
    void Inset(int dx, int dy);
    bool IntersectWith(const RectBounds *bounds);
    RectBounds *UnionWith(const RectBounds *bounds);
    void ClampWithin(int leftLimit, int topLimit,
                     int rightLimit, int bottomLimit);
    RectBounds *InsetByQuarter(RectBounds *outBounds) const;
    RectBounds *OutsetByHalf(RectBounds *outBounds) const;
};

void InitPointPair(Point *point, int y, int x);
void InitRectBounds(RectBounds *bounds, int left, int top, int right, int bottom);
void InitRectBoundsFromSize(RectBounds *bounds, int left, int top, int width, int height);
int RectArea(const RectBounds *bounds);
bool IntersectRects(const RectBounds *first, const RectBounds *second, RectBounds *result);
void UnionRects(const RectBounds *first, const RectBounds *second, RectBounds *result);
void OffsetRect(RectBounds *bounds, int dx, int dy);
void InsetRect(RectBounds *bounds, int dx, int dy);
bool PointInRect(int y, int x, const RectBounds *bounds);
bool IsRectEmptyOrInvalid(const RectBounds *bounds);
bool RectsEquivalent(const RectBounds *first, const RectBounds *second);
bool RectContainsRect(const RectBounds *inner, const RectBounds *outer);

#endif
